- The predefined batch mappings in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare_batches.py` only cover holistic dimensions.
- Those zeroed dimensions are defined in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/_shared/review_data/per_file_dimensions.json`, so they stay at `0.0` unless explicitly requested.

> AGENT

Yes. I audited it, and you’re right: it’s still too flat in key areas.

**Current hotspots**
1. `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify` has 31 direct files.
2. `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands` has 30 direct files.
3. `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors` has 21 direct files.
4. `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review` has 20 direct files.
5. `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/output` has 16 direct files.
6. `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors` has 18 direct files.
7. `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors` has 15 direct files.

Keeping tests both at repo top-level and inside package is fine. I’d keep that.

**Target structure**
1. Keep top-level `desloppify` thin: entrypoints + registry + shared primitives only.
2. Group command internals by command family.
3. Group detectors by concern domain.
4. Split review into context/dimensions/import/prepare/remediation domains.
5. Split output into scorecard/tree/viz domains.
6. Keep language plugin-local tests in `desloppify/lang/*/tests`; keep cross-system tests in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests`.

**Concrete reorg plan**
1. **Phase 1: Commands package split**
   - Move `review_*` modules into `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/review/`.
   - Move `status*` and `_status_*` into `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/status/`.
   - Move `next*` into `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next/`.
   - Keep one thin command entry module per command at `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/`.

2. **Phase 2: Detectors domain split**
   - Create:
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/structure/`
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/quality/`
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/security/`
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/hygiene/`
   - Move files accordingly; keep `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/__init__.py` as registry facade.

3. **Phase 3: Review domain split**
   - Create:
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/context/`
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/dimensions/`
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/importing/`
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/preparation/`
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/remediation/`
   - Leave thin compatibility exports in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/__init__.py` for one release only.

4. **Phase 4: Output split**
   - Create:
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/output/scorecard/`
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/output/tree/`
     - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/output/viz/`
   - Move `_scorecard_*`, `scorecard_*`, `tree_*`, `_viz_*` into those packages.

5. **Phase 5: Top-level package cleanup**
   - Move scoring modules into one package namespace.
   - Move state modules into one package namespace.
   - Move work-queue and policy modules into dedicated subpackages.
   - Remove `.DS_Store` under `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/.DS_Store`.

6. **Phase 6: Language folder flattening**
   - Split TS and Python detector folders by concern (deps/smells/security/etc.).
   - Mirror structure in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/tests` and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/tests`.

7. **Phase 7: Guardrails**
   - Add a CI check that flags directories with too many direct files (e.g., >12 for production modules).
   - Update `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/STRUCTURE.md` to match reality.

**Execution style**
1. Do this in 5-7 small PRs, not one mega-change.
2. Use `git mv` and immediate import rewrites in each PR.
3. Run targeted tests per moved domain before the next PR.

If you want, I can start Phase 1 now and actually perform the moves for `commands/review`, `commands/status`, and `commands/next` in one focused pass.

> DEVELOPER

Wait, why didn't move do all those updates?

> AGENT

Because I used bulk `git mv` first, not `desloppify move` per file/directory. `desloppify move` only rewrites references when it performs each move itself, so batched raw renames bypassed that rewrite step.

There’s a second limitation too: `desloppify move` focuses on code import references for supported language graphs, not every Python dotted import/test patch string across the repo. I’m now doing the missing rewrite pass explicitly.

You’re right that navigation was too flat in key areas.  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/` is already the Python “main subfolder” (package root), so the better fix is to split crowded folders into domain subpackages, not add an extra wrapper layer.

I implemented that:

- Review modules split into domain folders:
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/dimensions/`
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/importing/`
- Scorecard internals split out of `output/`:
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/output/scorecard_parts/`
- Import rewrites and callsites were updated accordingly (including tests and scan/status wiring).

Current structure impact:

- `review/` root files: `19 -> 8`
- `output/` root files: `16 -> 7`
- `flat_dirs` findings remain clear.

Validation I ran:

- Focused review+split tests: `76 passed`
- Focused scorecard/direct-coverage tests: `58 passed`
- CLI import smoke: passed
- Scan smoke: `.venv/bin/desloppify scan --path . --skip-slow` completed successfully

There are still existing unrelated failures in the broader review suite (not introduced by this folder split) that we can clean next.

Next high-impact reorg passes are obvious:

1. Reduce top-level root files (`plan.py`, `state.py`, `scoring.py`, `work_queue.py`, `zones.py`) into domain subpackages.
2. Split crowded `commands/` into clearer groups (`core`, `workflow`, `reporting`).
3. Add package-level index docs for each domain folder so new contributors can grok flow in under 2 minutes.

> DEVELOPER

was that a bug with desloppify move

> AGENT

Partly.

- The **first misses** were not a `move` bug: I had used `git mv` in an earlier pass, so `desloppify move` rewrite logic never ran.
- But in this pass, there **is** a real `desloppify move` gap:
  - It missed imports like `from ...output import scorecard_projection as ...` when moving `scorecard_projection.py`.
  - It also doesn’t preflight module/package name collisions (e.g. moving into `review/dimensions/` while `review/dimensions.py` existed), which caused a temporary import break.

So yes: there are legit `move` edge-case bugs/limitations. I manually fixed the affected imports, but the `move` engine itself still needs those pattern guards added.

Yes. The current package is much better, but one more abstraction layer would make it easier to navigate.

Recommended top-level abstractions under `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify`:

1. `app/` for user-facing entrypoints  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli_support`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/output`

2. `engine/` for scan + scoring execution core  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/planning`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/policy`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/state_internal`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring_internal`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue_internal`

3. `intelligence/` for higher-level reasoning signals  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/narrative`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/integrity`

4. `plugins/` (or keep `lang/`) for language implementations  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang`

Most sensible abstraction boundary: `app` (how users interact) vs `engine` (how scores/findings are produced) vs `intelligence` (subjective/review reasoning).  
That split makes ownership and onboarding much clearer.

Implemented. I used the existing holistic `package_organization` dimension as the directory-structure subjective detector, then made it first-class and low-weight.

What I changed:

- Enabled structure review by default for shared language profiles (TS/C# now include it):
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_shared/review_dimensions.py`
- Added low-but-meaningful subjective weight (`5.0`, about half of average subjective weight):
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/policy/core.py`
- Exposed it with clearer display label:
  - `package_organization -> Structure Nav`
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/subjective/core.py`
- Ensured `scan --reset-subjective` resets this dimension too:
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py`
- Strengthened the holistic prompt rubric for this dimension to force objective evidence and staged reorg plans:
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_shared/review_data/holistic_dimensions.json`
- Strengthened batch prompts so when `package_organization` is in-scope, reviewers must use `holistic_context.structure` signals and include move-order + validation steps:
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_core.py`
- Fixed an unrelated blocker discovered during validation (`review --run-batches` NameError):
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/cmd.py`

Tests/verification run:

- `6 passed` targeted tests for new dimension behavior/scoring/reset/display.
- `5 passed` targeted review batch flow tests (`run_batches`/packet prep paths).
- Runtime check:
  - `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --path . --only-batches 1 --dry-run` now works.

How to guide users through this process now:

1. Prepare only structure review:
   - `cd /user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify`
   - `.venv/bin/desloppify review --prepare --path . --dimensions package_organization`
2. Run focused batch import flow:
   - `.venv/bin/desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --path . --only-batches 1 --scan-after-import`
3. Check score impact:
   - `.venv/bin/desloppify status`
4. If you want a clean rerun baseline:
   - `.venv/bin/desloppify scan --path . --reset-subjective`

This now gives you a structured, objective-evidence-driven folder/layout subjective review with deliberately lower weighting but real score influence.

> DEVELOPER

And what do you mean underweighted? How are the language specific subjective items even weighted?

> AGENT

“Underweighted” meant exactly this: the directory-structure dimension was effectively getting the fallback weight `1.0`, while core subjective dimensions had much larger explicit weights (`12`, `22`, etc.).

How subjective weighting works now:

- Overall formula:
  - `overall = 0.4 * mechanical_avg + 0.6 * subjective_avg`
  - Implemented in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/results/core.py`
- Subjective pool formula:
  - `subjective_avg = sum(score_i * weight_i) / sum(weight_i)`
  - Also in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/results/core.py`
- Weight source:
  - Global map in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/policy/core.py` (`SUBJECTIVE_DIMENSION_WEIGHTS`)
  - If a dimension is not listed, it defaults to `1.0` (`_subjective_dimension_weight` fallback)

Language-specific part:

- Languages choose which holistic dimensions are in scope (e.g. TS/C#/Python lists) via their review config (`holistic_review_dimensions`), e.g.:
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/typescript/review.py`
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/review.py`
- But weights are **not per-language** today; they are global once a dimension is present.
- Scorecard language policies in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/output/scorecard_parts/dimension_policy.py` are display/order only, not scoring weight.

For the structure dimension specifically:

- `package_organization` now displays as `Structure Nav` in
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/subjective/core.py`
- It now has explicit weight `5.0` in
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/policy/core.py`

So previously it behaved like a small “default-weight” signal; now it has deliberate but still modest influence.

> AGENT

Short answer: not yet. It is **partly structured**, but extension UX is still brittle.

- But a new dimension still requires touching multiple manual maps: weights (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/policy/core.py:156`), display labels (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/subjective/core.py:9`), reset list (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py:23`), scorecard policy (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/output/scorecard_parts/dimension_policy.py:16`), and often batch mapping (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare_batches.py:138`).

> DEVELOPER

And can we weight the language specific subjective questions as well? And if so, is there guidance on how to weight them and have it all worked for the overall system?

> AGENT

Yes.

You can weight language-specific subjective dimensions now by setting `meta.weight` in the language override prompt entry, e.g. in:

- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/<lang>/review_data/per_file_dimensions.override.json`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/<lang>/review_data/holistic_dimensions.override.json`

Use:

- `meta.enabled_by_default: true` to auto-register it
- `meta.weight: <number>` for scoring weight
- `meta.display_name: "<label>"` for output label
- `meta.reset_on_scan: true|false` for `scan --reset-subjective`

How it affects the whole system:

- Overall score is `40% mechanical + 60% subjective`.
- Inside subjective, contribution is proportional to weight:  
  `dimension share = weight / sum(subjective weights for that scan language)`.

So yes, language-specific weights are supported and flow into overall scoring correctly.

Practical weighting guidance:

- `1-3`: niche/minor signal
- `3-6`: important but not dominant
- `6-10`: core language-specific quality axis
- `>10`: only if you intentionally want strong dominance

One caveat:

- The compact scorecard list is still curated in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/output/scorecard_parts/dimension_policy.py`, so a new dimension can affect overall score even if it is not shown in that condensed panel.

> AGENT

Yes to all three.

1. Supported + documented  
- It is supported in code, and I documented it clearly in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/README.md` under:
  - `Adding Subjective Dimensions (One Place)`
  - `How Language-Specific Dimensions Behave`
  - `Weighting Guidance`

2. Override vs add behavior  
- **Override existing**: reuse the same `dimension_prompts.<key>` in language override JSON; prompt/meta (including `weight`) overrides for that language.

1. Keep canonical tests in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests`.
2. Keep in-package tests only when they must live with a language plugin (very small smoke/contract set), e.g. under `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/*/tests`.

4. Simplify pytest discovery in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/pyproject.toml` once duplicates are removed.

So: not “everything inside package”; use top-level as primary, package-local only where colocating genuinely helps.

> DEVELOPER

So why not have them inside the package?

> AGENT

Because in this repo, “inside package” means “shipped to users.”

With your current config in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/pyproject.toml` (`include = ["desloppify*"]`), moving all tests under `desloppify/...` would:

1. Put test code into the wheel/install.
2. Increase package size and install noise.
3. Blur runtime vs dev-only code boundaries.
4. Make accidental imports/path coupling more likely.

Top-level `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests` keeps tests as dev assets by default, which is cleaner for a CLI package.

If you want everything in-package anyway, that’s doable, but then we should explicitly decide whether to ship tests or add exclusions so they stay out of distribution artifacts.

> AGENT

The 6 skips are intentional Roslyn integration/parity tests, gated by an env var that isn’t set.

- Skip gate: `DESLOPPIFY_TEST_CSHARP_ROSLYN_CMD`
- Skipped tests are in:
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/lang/csharp/test_csharp_deps.py`
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/test_csharp_deps.py`
- Each file has 3 Roslyn-gated tests, so `3 + 3 = 6`.

To unskip them, set the command and rerun tests, e.g.:

```bash
export DESLOPPIFY_TEST_CSHARP_ROSLYN_CMD="your-roslyn-command {path}"
.venv/bin/pytest -q -rs
```

`{path}` is optional; if omitted, the repo path gets appended automatically.
